FRAMEWORK required to use sources that are validated by the Responsible Minerals Initiative (RMI) or any other business
initiative recognized by the OECD.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
(ESRS 2) Outflows
The Company is pursuing the goal of designing the packaging portfolio for recycling or reuse. Our business
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
unit Consumer Brands is working on improving the biodegradability of the ingredients in our formulations.
Solutions from the Adhesive Technologies business unit help to reduce resource consumption in the use
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
phase. They increase the potential for reparability and can contribute to extending the service life of prod-
WATER AND MARINE ucts and technologies. Henkel prioritizes the use of sustainable materials such as recycled resources for
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) packaging and the use of renewable resources as the basis for our ingredients. The Company is actively
pursuing a strategy to minimize waste and support the transition to a circular economy.
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
Henkel promotes the integration of circular economy principles within operational practices for packaging,
raw materials and products. This includes maximizing resource efficiency and minimizing resource consump-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) tion, while also enhancing stakeholder engagement by fostering collaboration with our suppliers and cus-
tomers.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
Waste
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS Henkel embraces circular design principles, with a strong emphasis on enhancing design-for-recycling and
(ESRS S2) uptake of recycled materials in packaging. Henkel strives to reduce waste from its own production facilities
as well as upstream and downstream value chain facilities. Our goal is to reduce waste at the source by mak-
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
ing production processes more efficient. This requires refining manufacturing techniques, minimizing the
(ESRS S3)
use of raw materials, reducing by-product formation and implementing lean manufacturing practices to
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS generate less waste overall.
(ESRS S4)
Henkel’s objectives include compliance with environmental regulations and international conventions specif-
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1) ically related to the management of mercury, persistent organic pollutants (POPs) and hazardous waste,
as well as compliance with the prohibitions and requirements of the Minamata Convention, the Stockholm
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION Convention and the Basel Convention.

Sustainable sourcing and use of renewable resources (E5-1_15b)
FOREWORD
Through its supplier management approach, Henkel supports the sustainable procurement of renewable
SCOPE AND REFERENCE resources, thereby enabling and promoting their use. As part of Henkel´s supplier management activities,
FRAMEWORK Henkel collaborates intensively with its strategic suppliers to ensure the procurement of sustainable raw
materials. Henkel uses the Sustainable Palm Index (SPI) to assess the progress of its own suppliers with
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
respect to their level of supply chain knowledge, sustainable sourcing practices and compliance with the
(ESRS 2)
NDPE (No Deforestation, No Peat, No Exploitation) principles. Henkel supports the physical transformation
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) toward the use of certified products in the industry and the shift in the market toward certified sustainable
palm (kernel) oil products. A multi-stakeholder approach is at the center of Henkel’s work on a sustainable
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) palm and palm kernel oil economy, which also includes their derivatives. When procuring paper, we look for
independent certificates such as PEFC, SFI, FSC or similar, where possible. As part of Henkel’s supplier survey,
WATER AND MARINE
the quantities of certified paper and cardboard packaging are recorded annually through self-declaration.

Use of ingredients made from renewable raw materials
BIODIVERSITY AND
Henkel strives for a responsible management of raw materials, and especially the conservation of natural
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
resources and biodiversity. The Company uses ingredients based on renewable raw materials to optimize the
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR overall characteristics of its own products, wherever this is compatible with environmental, economic and
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) social considerations. Due to the complexity and scalability of chemical production processes, we see the mass
balance approach as an opportunity to promote this transformation.

E5-T1: The target level is 100 percent. The target is absolute and measured as a percentage.

E5-T2: The target level is over 30 percent. The target is absolute and measured as a percentage.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) E5-T3: The target level is -50 percent vs. 2010. The target is relative and measured as a percentage.

consideration given to the perspectives of external stakeholders. Steps including a stakeholder survey were
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS taken to ensure this.

SCOPE AND REFERENCE product durability plays a minor role. Repairability is not relevant for products placed on the market by

tent has no recyclable share. Information about the recyclable share of product packaging is provided in

CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Waste streams (E5-5_38a)
Waste is defined as any material, substance or object that leaves a Henkel site for disposal or treatment.
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
We currently divide our waste into six main categories:
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) 1. Production waste – Waste from production itself, including obtaining and handling raw materials and
packaged materials used in production, staging areas, shipping preparation, outbound goods and
BIODIVERSITY AND Henkel warehouses for raw materials and packaged materials.
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
2. Obsolete product waste – Waste that consists of delisted and expired materials, expired labels, fragrances,
promotional materials and products.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) 3. Waste from wastewater systems – By-products from wastewater treatment.
4. Municipal waste – General waste from office areas, general facilities and other non-production-related
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) areas within a production site.
5. Construction waste – Waste from limited-duration projects related to renovations, expansions or demolitions.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
6. High Quality Outbound Recycling (HQOR) – Waste where the quality of the material is maintained or
(ESRS S2)
completely restored. The material replaces raw materials or demonstrably fully reintroduces them into
the raw material market.

Methods for data calculation and assumptions (E5-5_40, AR 33)
FOREWORD
The total amount of products that Henkel produces is calculated on the basis of the net amount of finished
SCOPE AND REFERENCE products that leave a Henkel production site and is sold to customers or that is delivered to a co-packer for
FRAMEWORK packing/blister packaging. The total amount also includes semi-finished goods or mass-produced products
that leave our site for further production at a different Henkel site or a contract manufacturer. The total
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
amount of traded goods or finished products produced by a third party is not taken into account.
(ESRS 2)
Waste data is collected and reported monthly. Total waste is calculated as the sum of production waste,
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
obsolete product waste, waste from wastewater systems, municipal waste, construction waste and pure and
material-specific recyclable material waste.

Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model
(ESRS 2, SBM-3_48b)
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
Henkel is a production company with a large number of employees (around 47,150) at different sites world-
wide. It has a wide range of levers for improving the social conditions of the Company’s employees. How-
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) ever, it also has to deal with the challenges posed by the different labor and social legislation in the various
countries.
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) During the materiality assessment, we identified potential positive and negative impacts with regard to the
Company’s workforce. The positive impacts include the creation of good working conditions and the promo-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
tion of equal treatment and equal opportunities, improving the well-being of employees, increasing produc-
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
tivity and job satisfaction, and promoting health by reducing stress.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
By contrast, difficult working conditions can have a negative impact on the health and well-being of employ-
ees. These factors include the risk of accidents and illness, unregulated working hours combined with fatigue
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) and stress, inadequate financial remuneration or a lack of job security due to the absence of collective bar-
gaining power. An underrepresentation of women in the workforce, potential pay gaps and the neglect of
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES diversity, equity and inclusion (DEI) can lead to systemic discrimination and limit equal opportunities.
(ESRS S3)
Henkel’s corporate strategy, the Purposeful Growth Agenda, specifically emphasizes the importance of the
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS continuous development and sense of belonging of our employees worldwide. We are committed to promot-
(ESRS S4)
ing equal opportunities, strengthening diversity and respecting human rights in all our activities.

Policies related to own workforce (S1-1)
WATER AND MARINE Key content of the policy (S1-1_19; S1-1_20a; S1-1_24a, 24c, 24d; MDR-P_65a)
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
We are committed to respecting the rights and entitlements of employees and to providing fair working
conditions. In addition to the corresponding national laws, this also includes the policies set out below.
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
Working conditions
 Working time:
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) The maximum work time of a normal work week must usually not exceed 48 hours, unless otherwise per-
missible by law. Exceptions to this rule apply only where both of the following conditions exist: National
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) law allows work time exceeding this limit and a freely negotiated collective bargaining agreement is in
force that allows work time averaging, including adequate rest periods. All overtime work undertaken
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
must not exceed 12 hours per week. At least one day off must be granted per seven-day working period.
(ESRS S2)
 Adequate wages:
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3) Henkel is committed to creating fair working conditions, including remuneration in line with the market
that secures livelihoods. Compensation shall be reviewed on a regular basis and considering job perfor-
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS mance in a non-discriminatory manner. Furthermore, remuneration must be paid as agreed and without
(ESRS S4)
undue delay. Deductions from remuneration must be clearly stated.
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)

FOREWORD

FRAMEWORK employees.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
 Work-life balance:
(ESRS 2)
Henkel supports work-life balance through flexible working arrangements, adherence to working time
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) regulations and provisions for appropriate leave and parental leave. We believe that parental leave should
apply based on the caregiver role and not based on gender or biological parent status. As such all genders
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) as well as adoptive-, foster-, surrogacy-, and LGBTQ+-parents should be able to take parental leave. Any
local Henkel policies on parental leave comply with and are adopted in accordance with local rules and
WATER AND MARINE
regulations.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
BIODIVERSITY AND  Health and safety:
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) Henkel puts strong emphasis on ensuring the health and safety of employees and external workers. Pre-
venting health risks in the workplace is an integral part of every Henkel manager’s duties. Every employee
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
is responsible and accountable for health and safety within the scope of their authority. To ensure that
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
these principles are kept, workplace risk assessments must be performed and documented for all work-
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) places at appropriate intervals. As part of this assessment, hazards must be identified, risks must be as-
sessed and preventive and/or corrective measures must be implemented, followed up and checked for
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS their effectiveness. Necessary training must be conducted.
(ESRS S2)
Equal treatment and opportunities for all

(ESRS S3)

CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4) work for compensation. This should ensure that employees with similar roles and responsibilities receive
comparable pay. These practices support the reduction of discrepancies in salary decisions across the
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1) organization.

Measures to provide remedy for human rights impacts (S1-1_20c)
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
Henkel’s commitment to conducting all business in an ethical and legal manner is inextricably linked to our
BIODIVERSITY AND obligation to respect internationally recognized human rights. Henkel is therefore also responsible for iden-
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) tifying, analyzing and assessing the impacts of its business decisions. This also includes potential deleterious
effects on human rights and basic employee rights, as well as cases of corruption – and ensuring that, where
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
necessary, appropriate measures for remediation are duly initiated. This is to ensure that all human rights,
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
including those of local communities, are respected by all of Henkel’s business operations worldwide.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
Activities, products and processes that have or can have significant adverse social impacts, including potential
human rights risks, are identified and assessed by Henkel. In the event of changes in material circumstances,
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) such assessments must be appropriately reviewed. Henkel conducts risk analyses once a year and on an ad
hoc basis to identify potential human rights and environmental risks in its own business operations and in
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES the supply chain. When appraising new operations or new projects, potential human rights risks must be in-
(ESRS S3)
cluded within the associated risk assessments and in the corresponding decision-making procedures.
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
Indications of impact must be documented and made available to the relevant decision-makers to avoid or
(ESRS S4)
minimize impairment.

Form of employee engagement (S1-2_27a)
(ESRS S3)

as much detail as possible about topics such as the corporate strategy. Alternative mechanisms are in place
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS

BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1) employee assemblies or employee committees. These serve as voluntary, informal tools that enable close
dialog with management. In this way, we can communicate with our employees, incorporate their perspec-

tives at an early stage and shape future topics together. It also helps Henkel to avoid conflicts of interest and

Stages, types and frequency of engagement (S1-2_27b, AR 19)
FOREWORD
To promote collaboration between management and workers’ representatives at a European level, Henkel
SCOPE AND REFERENCE has voluntarily established a European Works Council (EWC). The EWC meets approximately five times a
FRAMEWORK year. There, Henkel informs the workers’ representatives of its European companies about matters such
as the economic situation, transnational initiatives of the Company such as restructuring or reorganization
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
programs, safety, health and the environment, and training and development programs.
(ESRS 2)
In Germany, Henkel also involves employee representatives in many decision-making processes beyond
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
those for which worker participation is a statutory requirement. The results of this decades-long, open and
constructive cooperation between employers and employee representatives include, for example, an im-
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
proved work-life balance, flexible working hours, accident prevention and health protection in the work-
WATER AND MARINE place, company retirement schemes and preventive health care.

& Home Care into the integrated Consumer Brands business unit. This restructuring process spans several

AFFECTED COMMUNITIES ment, we worked closely with the employee representative organizations in each country to identify socially
(ESRS S3)

Insight into the perspectives of vulnerable or marginalized groups (S1-2_28)
FOREWORD
At Henkel, we pursue a holistic diversity, equity and inclusion (DEI) approach. We recognize that many differ-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE ent characteristics contribute to our diversity. However, our strategic initiatives and actions focus on selected
FRAMEWORK core dimensions of diversity, including gender, international and ethnic origin, LGBTQ+, people with disabili-
ties and generational diversity. Henkel has nine global employee resource groups (ERGs), each focused on a
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
specific diversity dimension. To gain insights into the perspectives of our workforce, there is close collabora-
(ESRS 2)
tion between these ERGs and the DEI and HR teams. We also regularly analyze the extent to which these
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) groups are represented at Henkel, wherever data is available. Additionally, a monthly pulse-check survey is

POLLUTION (ESRS E2) equity and inclusion.

Effectiveness of the channels (S1-3_32e, AR 32)
FOREWORD
Henkel treats all reports with the utmost care, speed and confidentiality, and in full compliance with applica-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE ble privacy laws. Among other things, the confidentiality principle is strictly adhered to. This means that only
FRAMEWORK Henkel’s Compliance organization (local and global) and, if necessary, other departments required for the
investigation or external professional investigators or auditors have access to the report, the investigation
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
and the follow-up.
(ESRS 2)
The process is properly registered and documented. The investigations follow the principles of procedural
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
fairness and objectivity. They lead to an impartial decision, with all parties involved being presumed inno-
cent until a violation has been proven.
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)

WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) as needed. On our website, in the section Whistleblower Process and Compliance Hotline, we provide
procedural guidelines for the grievance mechanism. This document is available in more than 40 languages.

Key current and planned actions (S1-4_37; S1-4_38a; MDR-A_68a; AR 22; AR 42)
FOREWORD
Working conditions
SCOPE AND REFERENCE
FRAMEWORK  Working time:
We are standardizing time and attendance tracking at Henkel through the introduction of a new global
GENERAL DISCLOSURES payroll and timekeeping system. This system provides better oversight of compliance with our external
(ESRS 2)
and internal regulations on working hours. In addition, as part of our human rights due diligence approach,
our country organizations are required to regularly conduct risk assessments regarding working hours to
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
protect the rights and entitlements of employees.
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
 Adequate wages:
WATER AND MARINE Henkel is committed to creating fair working conditions, including market-appropriate and adequate
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
remuneration, performance-based bonuses, social security and other benefits. For employees in the low-
est pay categories, Henkel ensures that the legally applicable minimum wage is observed when setting
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) their salaries. We have also introduced annual reviews based on external benchmarks to assess an adequate
living wage. These analyses are carried out across all regions and business areas at Henkel. They apply to
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR all employees.
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
 Collective bargaining:
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
Henkel is a member of employer associations in various countries. In Germany, for example, Henkel is a
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS member of the German Chemical Industry Employers’ Association (BAVC) and adheres to industry-specific
(ESRS S2) collective bargaining agreements in Germany, which apply to all our collective bargaining employees at
our German sites.
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3)
 Work-life balance:
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS Wherever possible, Henkel offers flexible working hours, part-time work and sabbaticals to support employ-
(ESRS S4) ees in achieving a balanced work-life relationship. In addition to the flexible option allowing employees to
spend up to 40 percent of their working hours outside the office, we introduced a new global parental
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
leave directive in 2024. Regardless of role and in all countries where Henkel operates, we offer employees
who become parents a minimum of eight weeks of paid leave. This applies to adoptive-, foster-, surrogacy-
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
and LGBTQ+-parents of all genders.
HENKEL SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2024 248

FOREWORD

SCOPE AND REFERENCE tative actions and promptly investigate incidents. These actions support our goal of achieving a +60-per-
FRAMEWORK cent improvement in safety per one million working hours (2025 vs. 2010). Our health initiatives for
employees include digital platforms, peer coaching and training programs. For a more detailed descrip-
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
tion of the targets, see the section “Targets related to the company’s workforce“ (S1-5) in this chapter.

POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
ple of “equal pay for equal work” at Henkel. Based on this analysis, we are continuously developing solu-
WATER AND MARINE tions in collaboration with our Human Resources team to address identified gaps. To ensure fair and
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) competitive pay for our employees across all Henkel countries and departments, our salary bands are
regularly benchmarked against market standards.

SCOPE AND REFERENCE

FRAMEWORK
conditions by analyzing factors such as application rates, employee turnover and retention. Our Workforce
GENERAL DISCLOSURES Analytics team uses this data to draw conclusions about the efficiency and effectiveness of our initiatives.
(ESRS 2) With regard to health and safety, we have a centralized system. This tool enables us to monitor progress
toward our safety goals and ambitions, and to assess the effectiveness of our actions.

WATER AND MARINE

and equitable workplace.

Avoiding material negative impacts (S1-4_41, AR 37)
FOREWORD
Henkel’s processes are designed to avoid material negative impacts on the Company’s workforce. To this
SCOPE AND REFERENCE end, Henkel has established strict guidelines for ethical standards and labor practices. Regular internal and
FRAMEWORK external audits and evaluations are conducted to minimize the risk of negative impacts.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES Internal audits and assessments are conducted by the Corporate Audit team. This team’s primary task is to
(ESRS 2) provide independent and objective audit and advisory services with the aim of evaluating the effectiveness
of the Company’s risk management system, control mechanisms and governance and monitoring processes.
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Regular audits are carried out globally at our production and administrative sites, as well as at our contract
manufacturing operations and logistics centers, with the results reported to the Management Board and the
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
Audit Committee of the Supervisory Board.

Workers in the value chain (ESRS S2)
FOREWORD
SCOPE AND REFERENCE
FRAMEWORK Strategy
Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model
GENERAL DISCLOSURES (ESRS 2, SBM-3_48b)
(ESRS 2)
As a global company with a complex value chain, we bear a special responsibility to uphold labor and social
standards, as well as the human rights of value chain workers. Henkel has identified potential impacts in the
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
value chain that may negatively affect this group. Physical risks, the handling of machinery and the use of
hazardous materials and substances may result in work-related injuries, illnesses and accidents among value
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
chain workers. Child labor disrupts education and can harm children’s health under dangerous conditions.
WATER AND MARINE Value chain workers may also face coercion and forced labor under abusive and exploitative working condi-
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
tions. The supply chains for palm and palm kernel oil and conflict minerals, including gold, tantalum, tung-
sten, tin and mica minerals, are under particular scrutiny due to potential negative impacts.
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
We expect our suppliers to comply with the requirements of our Responsible Sourcing Policy. When select-
ing and collaborating with business partners, we consider their performance regarding safety, health, envi-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) ronment, social standards and fair business practices. A human rights due diligence process is an integral
part of this approach. Through our involvement in the initiative “Together for Sustainability – Chemical Supply
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) Chains for a Better World” (TfS), we focus on identifying synergies in the processes required to manage
complex supply chains, while promoting sustainability and optimizing dialog with our global business partners.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
Our collaboration with the civil society organization Solidaridad is a key component in shaping a sustainable
(ESRS S2)
palm and palm kernel oil industry that simultaneously improves the livelihoods of smallholder farmers and
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES promotes sustainable agricultural practices.
(ESRS S3)
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)

Relationship between the actual and potential impacts and the strategy or business model
FOREWORD
(ESRS 2, SBM-3_10a)
The cross-industry Code of Conduct of the German Association for Supply Chain Management, Procure-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE
FRAMEWORK ment, and Logistics (BME), based on the principles of the United Nations Global Compact, is applicable for
Henkel’s suppliers worldwide. This code outlines our expectations regarding labor practices, human rights
GENERAL DISCLOSURES and environmental responsibility. Management systems must be built on robust policies and effective due
(ESRS 2)
diligence mechanisms for the risk assessment to identify, prevent, mitigate and potentially remedy viola-
tions. The impacts and capabilities of individual suppliers must be taken into account. The Code of Conduct
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
is therefore applicable internationally and is linked to our contractual relationships with strategic suppliers
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) alongside the Responsible Sourcing Policy. Our Responsible Sourcing Policy is based on our global Corpo-
rate Standard Purchasing, our Safety, Health, and Environmental Protection (SHE) standards and our Social
WATER AND MARINE Standards, which ensure that the interests of supplier companies are safeguarded and their rights and ex-
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
pectations are considered.
BIODIVERSITY AND Henkel’s Responsible Sourcing Policy is a key element of our commitment to sustainability leadership. This
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
commitment drives our daily actions, guiding our corporate strategy, the Purposeful Growth Agenda and
our Responsible Sourcing Agenda.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
Scope in relation to workers in the value chain (ESRS 2, SBM-3_11, 11a, 11a i-a v)
The principles set out by the Responsible Sourcing Policy are applicable throughout the value chain con-
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
nected to the key sub-topics of health and safety, child labor and forced labor.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) Value chain workers are defined as individuals not employed by Henkel and not directly under the supervision
of Henkel (for example, workers in raw material production, packaging or contract manufacturing). Value
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES chain workers produce defined work products as employees of a third party (and therefore under the super-
(ESRS S3)
vision of that third party). We continuously work to enhance transparency and understanding of our complex
value chain.
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
HENKEL SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2024 265
We are aware of the heightened likelihood of potential negative impacts in specific value chains for certain
FOREWORD
groups of workers. Value chain workers who may be significantly affected include those employed by com-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE panies in the upstream value chain whose activities are associated with specific raw materials, such as palm
FRAMEWORK and palm kernel oil, the “3TG” metals tin, tantalum and tungsten, as well as mica minerals. These materials
are associated with elevated risks. The primary source of these risks lies in the cultivation or extraction of
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
these raw materials, typically on plantations (in the case of palm and palm kernel oil) or through mining activi-
(ESRS 2)
ties. Beyond these upstream risks contact with hazardous chemicals, dangerous processes and working con-
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) ditions of workers in the downstream value chain can lead to work-related injuries, illnesses and accidents.
Workers in the downstream value chain are employees of organizations involved in the processes and activi-
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) ties necessary to ensure that products reach consumers and end-users, such as logistics providers, retailers
and distributors.
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
Geographical areas with significant risks of child or forced labor (ESRS 2, SBM-3_11b)
Potential impacts related to forced labor and child labor in our value chain concerning the “3TG” metals
BIODIVERSITY AND
tin, tantalum and tungsten, as well as mica minerals, palm and palm kernel oil have been identified in the
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
following countries: Bolivia, Brazil, China, India, Indonesia, Malaysia and Peru.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) Disclosures in case of material negative impacts (ESRS 2, SBM-3_11c)
Potential impacts were identified based on information from public sources and do not stem directly from
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) Henkel’s own findings. Sources include publications by the Responsible Mineral Initiative, the Responsible
MICA Initiative and the Roundtable on Sustainable Palm Oil (RSPO). These impacts occur very early in the
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS upstream value chain and pertain to individual incidents. Due to the complex nature of the chemical value
(ESRS S2)
chain, there may be minimal direct interaction with our Company. For further information, please refer to the
section above, “Geographical areas with significant risk of child or forced labor“ (SBM-3_11b).

Development of understanding for potentially vulnerable individuals (ESRS 2, SBM-3_12)
FOREWORD
A systematic risk analysis as part of our due diligence approach has given us an understanding of vulnerable
SCOPE AND REFERENCE value chain workers who may be at greater risk of harm.
FRAMEWORK
As part of our regular risk analysis, which we conduct for our own business activities, our supply chain and
GENERAL DISCLOSURES our services and products, we identify issues and risks that we consider to be a priority based on their poten-
(ESRS 2)
tial severity and our ability to influence them. This includes defining the risk potential in our procurement
markets and evaluating value chains on a cross-industry basis. In doing so, we focus on countries that inter-
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
national institutions have classified as risk countries. Based on this, we define the riskiest markets by combining
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) this analysis of risk countries with issues of particular relevance to our supply chains at that time and take
appropriate actions. In addition to self-assessment tools, we use externally audited instruments to validate
WATER AND MARINE and supplement the information provided by suppliers, enhancing the reliability and depth of our evaluations.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
This approach allows us to identify areas for improvement and potential risks more effectively. We actively
participate in industry initiatives and the development of standards to assess supplier performance and align
BIODIVERSITY AND
our evaluation criteria with best practices. By leveraging established frameworks and joining joint industry
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
initiatives, we promote transparency, accountability and continuous improvement among our supplier com-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR panies, fostering a culture of responsible and sustainable business practices. For further information on the
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
key types of value chain workers who may be negatively impacted, please refer to the section “Scope in relation
to workers in the value chain” (SBM-3_11) in this chapter.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)

Impact, risk and opportunity management
FOREWORD
Policies related to workers in the value chain (S2-1)
SCOPE AND REFERENCE Key content of the policy (S2-1_16, MDR-P_65a, AR 10)
FRAMEWORK Henkel is committed to respecting internationally acknowledged human rights for the workers in its value
chain and therefore requires suppliers to ensure fair working conditions and respect for human rights. In this
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
context, policies emphasizing health and safety provisions as well as the prevention of child and forced labor
(ESRS 2)
are of particular importance.
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Our product safety or product stewardship concepts cover the safe transport, handling and disposal – in
addition to their end-use – ensuring the safety of value chain workers when handling Henkel products as
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
intended. We are committed to providing safe products of the highest quality, for the benefit of all our
WATER AND MARINE stakeholders.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
In this context, our most important expectations toward our suppliers are as follows:
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)  Suppliers must have management systems in place to identify, assess and mitigate risks to ensure health
and safety of people affected by their operations.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR  Suppliers do not tolerate child labor (according to the International Labour Organization [ILO]).
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
 All work is voluntary and suppliers do not permit or cause forced, bonded or indentured labor, slavery or
human trafficking.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
With this, we address the material impacts related to “working conditions” (specifically “health and safety”) in
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) our upstream and downstream value chain and “other work-related rights” (specifically “child labor” and
“forced labor) in our upstream value chain.
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3)
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
HENKEL SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2024 268
Henkel monitors its suppliers and conducts regular assessments as part of the responsible sourcing process.
FOREWORD
Henkel requests suppliers to complete assessments and/or disclose relevant policies and procedures, data
SCOPE AND REFERENCE and/or other information. In selected cases, Henkel also conducts on-site audits of suppliers’ business oper-
FRAMEWORK ations to check the conformity of business activities with the specified requirements. Whenever Henkel requests
a supplier to undertake reasonable specific measures to prevent or end any breach, the supplier must document
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
the measures taken to ensure compliance and, upon request, provide Henkel with adequate information
(ESRS 2)
about these measures.
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
The effectiveness of preventive measures and remedial actions and the complaints procedure as well as
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) the material impacts, risks and opportunities and their changes are regularly reviewed by the Procurement
Committee, the Sustainability Council – and Compliance and Risk Committee, if appropriate.
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) Scope (S2-1_16, MDR-P_65b)
The provisions in this policy apply to the Henkel Group worldwide, including all operations and subsidiaries
BIODIVERSITY AND as well as their upstream and downstream value chain. No business units, regions, specific activities or stake-
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
holders are excluded.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR We expect our suppliers and other business partners to comply with the principles set out here and to im-
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
plement appropriate processes to respect human rights. Henkel’s Responsible Sourcing approach is global
in scope and applies to suppliers worldwide, operating in the areas of raw material, packaging, contract
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
manufacturers and traded goods, as well as indirect materials, and services and logistics.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
Accountability for the implementation (S2-1_16, MDR-P_65c)
(ESRS S2)
The Henkel Management Board bears overall responsibility for our sustainability concepts and policies. The
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES Sustainability Council aligns on common positions, prior to approval by the Management Board. These include
(ESRS S3) new strategic priorities, targets as well as risks and opportunities with respect to value chain workers. The
Sustainability Council approves specific policies addressing value chain workers and sets up topic-specific
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
project groups to steer the implementation of action plans, the compliance with common standards, the
(ESRS S4)
exchange of best practices, and the monitoring of the results. It also handles cross-departmental projects
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1) and continuously reviews their progress. The Chief Procurement Officer (CPO) and the Procurement Com-
mittee are responsible for all procurement activities and for ensuring responsible supply chain management.

Commitment to third-party standards or initiatives (S2-1_16, MDR-P_65d)
FOREWORD
Our codes and standards also reflect our commitment to the Universal Declaration of Human Rights, the Interna-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE tional Labour Organization’s (ILO) Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work, the Conventions of
FRAMEWORK the International Labour Organization, the International Covenant on Economic, Social and Cultural Rights, the
International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights, the United Nations Global Compact, as well as the expectations
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
set out in the United Nations Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights. The United Nations Guiding Prin-
(ESRS 2)
ciples include key elements such as frameworks and policies, risk analysis, effective measures, and reporting and
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) complaints mechanisms, which we have incorporated into our approach to respecting human rights. In addition,
the OECD Due Diligence Guidance for Responsible Business Conduct acts as a benchmark for our approach, which
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) we continuously review and develop as necessary.
WATER AND MARINE Our expectation toward our suppliers includes the ethical principles of the cross-industry Code of Conduct of the
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) German Association of Materials Management, Purchasing, and Logistics (BME) as well as our concept for respon-
sible procurement, which is based on our globally applicable Corporate Purchasing Standard, our SHE standards
BIODIVERSITY AND
and our Social Standards. Sustainable and ethical practice is an essential element of Henkel’s commitment to the
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
United Nations Global Compact, which comprises ten principles in the areas of human rights, labor rights, the
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR environment and anti-corruption.
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
In cases where international human rights are restricted by local laws, we strive to promote the principles behind
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) international standards without conflicting with local laws. Where local laws exceed international standards, we will
comply with these laws.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) Consideration of stakeholders (S2-1_16, MDR-P_65e)
Our suppliers and supply chain partners play an essential role in our sustainability transformation. Intensive
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
dialog and close cooperation are the basis to develop and maintain ethical and sustainable relationships
(ESRS S3)
with them. In this spirit, we carefully consider our suppliers’ performance, commitment and continuous im-
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS provement when selecting and collaborating with them.
(ESRS S4)
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)

FOREWORD
key component of our sustainability management approach. This includes our customers, consumers, suppli-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE ers, business partners, employees, shareholders, investors, neighbors and local communities, associations
FRAMEWORK and non-governmental organizations (NGOs), academia, as well as politicians and government authorities.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES Henkel is working with various cross-industry organizations, certification bodies and NGOs to ensure responsible
(ESRS 2)
sourcing. In the case of the palm oil industry, for example, this includes Action for Sustainable Derivatives

our partnerships with civil society organizations, such as Solidaridad, Henkel employees participate in field
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) visits to broaden their perspectives beyond the immediate business context and gain insights into the working
environment and interests of value chain workers. The knowledge gained through these partnerships is also
WATER AND MARINE incorporated into the design of existing concepts.

Commitment relating to human rights (S2-1_17)
FOREWORD
Our supplier base constitutes of companies with workers around the world and is of high relevance since the
SCOPE AND REFERENCE intensive dialog and close cooperation with our suppliers are essential for achieving sustainable business,
FRAMEWORK process and production practices. Our responsible sourcing approach focuses on these sustainability aspects
along our supply chains: environment, labor and human rights, ethics and sustainable procurement. These
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
main focal areas encompass the different topics we engage on with our suppliers and also serve as the core
(ESRS 2)
of our assessments and audit approach.
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Henkel has a clear process for taking remedial action: If Henkel discovers that a violation of a human-rights-
related or environmental obligation has occurred or is imminent at a supplier, it takes appropriate remedial
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
action(s) without undue delay. The measures are taken on a case-by-case basis, depending on the nature of
WATER AND MARINE the violation.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
Henkel has a clearly regulated complaints procedure: Possible compliance violations or human rights viola-
BIODIVERSITY AND tions in terms of actual or suspected breaches shall be reported as soon as possible to Henkel’s Compliance
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
Department. Whistleblowers are offered various channels to report potential misconduct. In addition to
employees, third parties can also use our compliance hotline or alternative reporting channels.

VALUE CHAIN WORKERS forced labor, trafficking in human beings and child labor. Compliance with the cross-sector BME code of
(ESRS S2) conduct is mandatory for Henkel’s suppliers worldwide.

GENERAL DISCLOSURES The Responsible Sourcing process is an integral part of our procurement activities. This process is initiated
(ESRS 2) before any new collaboration begins. It involves a recurring cycle of review, analysis and continuous improve-
ment with existing suppliers. We have further developed and expanded this as part of our cross-functional
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
and cross-business unit task force on human rights due diligence.
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) A central element is an assessment or audit at the start of a new business relationship. Authorized supplier
representatives are asked to either disclose existing sustainability performance results or complete a ques-
WATER AND MARINE
tionnaire that provides transparency regarding their sustainability performance, including labor and human
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
rights issues. We do this for the significant portion of our external purchasing volume using an assessment
BIODIVERSITY AND methodology developed by EcoVadis.
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)

RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR (including child and forced labor), employee standards and anti-corruption. The EcoVadis methodology is
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) based on international sustainability standards (UNGC, GRI and ISO norms) and is continuously updated.
This methodology builds on 21 indicators across four main categories: Environment, Labor and human
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
rights, Ethics and Sustainable procurement.

(ESRS S2)

AFFECTED COMMUNITIES performance and risks. The INX questionnaire on human rights and labor asks authorized representatives of
(ESRS S3) suppliers in the value chain to answer questions regarding their commitment to combating forced and child
labor, eliminating discrimination and ensuring good working conditions.

aimed at maximizing certified volumes. We also work closely with stakeholders along the value chain to pro-

SCOPE AND REFERENCE Areas, we conduct appropriate due diligence on our supply chain. We require our supplier companies to

Multi-stakeholder and industry initiatives play a crucial role in protecting the rights of value chain workers.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR This includes combating health and safety risks, child labor and forced labor through close collaboration, but
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
is not limited to these areas.
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
The Together for Sustainability (TfS) initiative plays a central role here. As a large number of TfS members
are also direct suppliers of Henkel and these suppliers also assess and audit their suppliers, we achieve a
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
more in-depth approach (tier n) to sustainability practices along the value chain. Henkel is actively involved
(ESRS S2)
in several workstreams of TfS. We are also a founding member of the Action for Sustainable Derivatives
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES (ASD) initiative. The initiative brings together organizations from across the palm (kernel) oil derivative sup-
(ESRS S3) ply chain to support efforts for greater transparency, adherence to the NDPE principles (No Deforestation,
No Peat, No Exploitation) and to drive positive change in the palm (kernel) oil industry.
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)

Partnership with the civil society organization Solidaridad
FOREWORD
Since 2013, Henkel has partnered with Solidaridad on initiatives in Colombia, Ghana, Honduras, Indonesia,
SCOPE AND REFERENCE Mexico, Nicaragua and Nigeria. The focus is on training in best practices, including climate-friendly agricul-
FRAMEWORK ture, influencing policy issues and engaging stakeholders. Together with farmers, we work to increase the
production volumes of sustainable, deforestation-free palm (kernel) oil and support their transition to agro-
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
forestry. This project includes collaboration with local government authorities, forest management and
(ESRS 2)
communities that play a key role in forestry in the project region.

Affected communities (ESRS S3)
FOREWORD
SCOPE AND REFERENCE
FRAMEWORK Strategy
Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model
GENERAL DISCLOSURES (ESRS 2, SBM-3_48b)
(ESRS 2)
Henkel’s products require a wide range of raw materials whose extracting involves land use and ecosystem
utilization. This may potentially adversely impact people living and working in areas where raw material
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
extraction takes place. These potential negative impacts were confirmed through the materiality assessment
based on various impact dimensions. For instance, the extraction of raw materials such as palm (kernel) oil
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
can affect the economic, cultural and social rights of indigenous peoples, potentially undermining the princi-
WATER AND MARINE ple of Free, Prior and Informed Consent (FPIC). Expansion of activities in the downstream value chain could
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
also infringe upon these rights in various ways.
BIODIVERSITY AND We are committed to respecting human rights enshrined in international frameworks and conventions. Derived
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
from this commitment, we also respect local, national and international land, water and resource rights,
including the rights of indigenous communities and FPIC-related rights. A key element of implementing this
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) commitment is our six-step responsible sourcing process, which integrates our suppliers worldwide into our
risk management and compliance strategy in relation to fulfilling human rights due diligence obligations. A
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) significant component of our actions is our engagement in multi-stakeholder and industry initiatives to better
understand and mitigate potential impacts on affected communities. The key activities include the partner-
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS

Impact, risk and opportunity management
FOREWORD
Policies related to affected communities (S3-1)
SCOPE AND REFERENCE Key contents of the policy (S3-1_14, MDR-P_65a)
FRAMEWORK Henkel is committed to doing business in an ethical and legal manner. This is inseparably linked with our
commitment to respecting internationally acknowledged human rights. Henkel respects local, national and
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
international land, water and resource rights, including those of indigenous communities. Suppliers must
(ESRS 2)
respect land rights including collective and traditional rights of women, indigenous peoples and local
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) communities and other vulnerable groups that may be affected by their operations and sourcing practices.
Suppliers must not engage in any form of land-grabbing. If legally permitted land use changes are made, suppliers
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) are required to obtain the free, prior and informed consent (FPIC) of affected indigenous communities, as
well as any other forms of participation that are required by local law. This applies in particular if a local
WATER AND MARINE
community’s access to water or other resources is affected. Unlawful forced evictions are not permitted.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
With this, we address the material impacts related to the rights of indigenous peoples specifically, FPIC and
BIODIVERSITY AND
cultural rights in our upstream and downstream value chain.
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
The Sustainability Council regularly reviews the effectiveness of our policies and progress toward our ob-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) jectives, as well as the material impacts, risks and opportunities and their changes. Where relevant, the
Compliance and Risk Committee may also review these elements of our approach based on the initiative of
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) the Chief Sustainability Officer.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS Scope (S3-1_14, MDR-P_65b)
(ESRS S2) The provisions in this policy apply to the Henkel Group worldwide, including all operations and subsidiaries
as well as their upstream value chain. No business units, regions or specific activities are excluded.

GENERAL DISCLOSURES (ESRS 2, SBM-3_48b)
(ESRS 2)
Millions of consumers trust Henkel’s products. This trust forms the foundation of our Company’s reputation
and business success. Material impacts, risks and opportunities related to consumers and end-users are
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
therefore of critical importance to Henkel. The materiality assessment identified potentially positive and
negative impacts within the downstream value chain. A positive impact relates to providing consumers and
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
end-users with better decision-making capabilities in product selection through transparent product infor-
WATER AND MARINE mation. On the negative side, improper product use and lack of safety information in very rare cases can
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
lead to health risks for consumers and end-users.
BIODIVERSITY AND We are committed to providing consumers and end-users with accurate and high-quality product information.
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
Our actions are guided by compliance with local and global labeling regulations, as well as by a commitment
to transparency regarding product ingredients and sustainability profiles. To achieve this, we continuously
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) improve data quality through digital enhancements and invest in structured processes for sustainability as-
sessment across our entire portfolio. In this way, we aim to strategically position ourselves as a reliable part-
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) ner in sustainability for our industrial and retail customers, supporting them in achieving their sustainability
goals. To ensure the highest level of product safety during use, we implement rigorous quality control pro-
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
cedures, conduct thorough safety assessments and continuously monitor products throughout their lifecycle.
(ESRS S2)
We ensure that consumers and end-users receive accurate and comprehensive product information that

Relationship between the actual and potential impacts and the strategy or business model
FOREWORD
(ESRS 2, SBM-3_9a)
Henkel has identified and assessed the potential impacts, risks and opportunities related to consumers and/or
SCOPE AND REFERENCE
FRAMEWORK end-users as part of its comprehensive materiality assessment. When conducting the materiality assessment,
it was taken into account that Henkel sells directly and indirectly to consumers through our trade partners,
GENERAL DISCLOSURES while also engaging in B2B transactions with end-users. For further information on aspects related to work-
(ESRS 2)
ers in the value chain, please refer to the chapter “Workers in the value chain” (ESRS S2).
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
The identified impacts on consumers and end-users play a significant role in shaping and adjusting Henkel’s
strategy. By considering information-related impacts, Henkel provides transparent product details, enabling
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
consumers and end-users to make informed choices that best suit their needs, thereby enhancing consumer
trust. To mitigate potential health risks, we implement actions to ensure safety and compliance at every stage,
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) implement strict quality control procedures and conduct thorough safety evaluations. Prioritizing these as-
pects contributes to the overall trust and satisfaction of our consumers and end-users.
BIODIVERSITY AND
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) Scope in relation to consumers and end-users (SBM-3_10, 10a, 10b, 10c)
At Henkel, the terms “consumers” and “end-users” are used as follows:
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)  Consumers: Individuals who purchase our products either for their personal use or on behalf of a third
party who uses the product privately. The products are not intended for resale or professional purposes,
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
such as trade fairs or B2B transactions.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2)  End-users: Individuals who ultimately use our products, such as industrial customers, craftspeople, hair-
dressers and children.
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3) All consumers and end-users could potentially be affected by material impacts. However, we have identified
the following key groups:
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)  Consumers and/or end-users of products that may have serious, undesirable effects if the products are
not used as intended: We ensure these risks are effectively managed and communicated. The negative
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
impacts on the “personal safety of consumers and/or end-users” relate to individual incidents (for exam-
ple a defect associated with a specific product or a person failing to follow instructions properly).

FRAMEWORK Health and Safety
We are committed to providing safe products of the highest quality, for the benefit of all our stakeholders.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
Our products touch millions of people every day. We therefore ensure that products are safe for their in-
(ESRS 2)
tended use in terms of human health and the environment. We comply with requirements regarding health,
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) and safety and as well as labeling that are applicable to us in the regions we operate in. We constantly re-
view and assess our products to ensure that they remain compliant. Our product safety or product steward-
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) ship concepts cover the safe transport, handling and disposal of products in addition to their end-use and
ensuring the safety of workers in the value chain when handling Henkel products as intended. This policy is
WATER AND MARINE
monitored through the specific portfolio-steering initiatives in each business unit.

ECONOMY (ESRS E5) oped with the A.I.S.E. (European Association of Detergent and Cleaning Product Manufacturers), which pro-
vide clear instructions on our packaging for the correct and safe use of our products. We strive to enable

our consumers and end-users to learn about the ingredients contained in our products. In line with this, we
are enhancing global transparency about the substances used and their respective safety. We also want to
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) help consumers use our products correctly and dispose of empty packaging, for example by placing special
recycling symbols or collection notices on packaging. We want to clearly communicate our sustainability
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES efforts, with an established and clear process to avoid greenwashing.

Ensuring the accuracy of product information
FOREWORD
We ensure that consumers and end-users worldwide receive accurate and comprehensive product infor-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE mation through highly qualified teams, processes and tools. The primary platform for managing this infor-
FRAMEWORK mation and data is the Product Information Management System (PIM), based on the SAP Hybris platform.
The PIM serves as a central repository for collecting, managing, enriching and distributing all product-
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
related information, including logistics, safety and R&D data, which are automatically sourced from the SAP
(ESRS 2)
system, as well as media links and uploads of marketing and sales data. This ensures a consistent and har-
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) monized information flow across the Company.
To provide consumers with the necessary information on product ingredients and safe use, key documents
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
such as technical data sheets (TDS), safety data sheets (SDS) and regulatory data sheets (RDS) are available.
WATER AND MARINE In the B2B sector, a complete safety data sheet (SDS) is provided when a new product is introduced, with all
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) changes proactively communicated to customers and end-users. Additionally, customers can contact the
Customer Service and Sales teams, as well as the Product Safety & Regulatory Affairs (PSRA) team for further
BIODIVERSITY AND
information. In the B2C sector, product details are available upon request or via the website, with key safety
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
information included on product labels. Specifically regarding the sub-topic of “Health & Safety,” the safety
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR data sheets and product labels provide clear instructions for the proper use of the products, as well as guid-
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) ance on handling in case of misuse of the product. Medical professionals with access to our SDS can provide
immediate assistance and advice to consumers.

Enhancing the transparency of our product portfolio’s sustainability performance
FOREWORD
Transparency is essential for assessing responsible action and sustainability performance. This applies inside
SCOPE AND REFERENCE and outside the Company. We are committed to continuously improving data quality and using digital tools
FRAMEWORK to enhance sustainability efforts globally. By implementing automated systems (based on SAP and other
Henkel IT tools) to capture and display comprehensive sustainability data for all products, we ensure that
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
our communication is credible and supported by reliable data.
(ESRS 2)
Internally, we assess the sustainability contribution of our portfolio through a structured process, categorizing
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
products based on their impact across the value chain. Products are classified into four categories: “Transitioner”
and “Performer” products contribute minimally to sustainability, “Contributor” products have a positive impact
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
and “Pioneer” products make an exceptionally positive contribution. This sustainability assessment aims to
WATER AND MARINE create transparency regarding the sustainability performance of our global portfolios and evaluate our efforts
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) toward more sustainable products.
BIODIVERSITY AND In addition to internal actions, we recognize the benefits of collaboration and collective impact. To raise con-
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
sumer awareness, our Consumer Brands business unit supported the development of the Cosmile app by
haut.de. The science-based platform allows consumers in selected European countries to scan the EAN bar
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) code of cosmetic products to obtain detailed information about the ingredients. In addition, to promote
sustainable consumption, in 2021 we entered into a partnership with Unilever, LVMH, L’Oréal, and Natura to
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) establish the Eco Beauty Score Consortium (EBS). More than 70 companies and associations worldwide have
now joined the EBS. The consortium aims to create a unified system for evaluating the environmental impact
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
of cosmetic products to help consumers make informed and sustainable purchasing decisions.

Identification of actions to address negative impacts (S4-4_32a)
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Although our products undergo numerous tests and assessments to ensure a high level of safety, unforeseen
deviations from our high quality standards may occur in very rare cases. In preparation for any such cases and
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
to ensure the best possible health and safety of consumers and end-users, in 2009 Henkel introduced a corpo-
WATER AND MARINE rate standard for potential product crises and product recalls that applies to all sites. An updated version of this
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) standard was made available Company-wide in 2020. Digital training on this topic has also been available in
multiple languages since the end of 2020. Henkel’s global corporate standard comes into effect when a prod-
BIODIVERSITY AND
uct incident presents a potential risk to the health or lives of customers, consumers and end-users. Its purpose
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
is to establish an organizational structure with binding procedures, clear responsibilities and guidelines for an
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR appropriate and prompt response to a potential product crisis or recall, ensuring the health and safety of cus-
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) tomers, consumers and end-users. The standard is based on five key elements and process steps:
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) 1. Product incident
If Henkel becomes aware of a product incident with potential risks to the health or life of customers
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
and consumers, information must be promptly gathered, and questions regarding the exact details of
(ESRS S2)
the incident, its cause, potential health impacts and frequency must be answered.
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3) 2. Product Crisis Team
The Product Crisis Team manages product incidents with potential health or life risks. It is essential to
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS inform team members in the event of a critical situation. The team consists of five mandatory members:
(ESRS S4)
Business Unit, Research and Development/Product Development, Legal Department, Supply Chain Man-
agement and Corporate Communications.
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
HENKEL SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2024 307
3. Risk Assessment and Classification
FOREWORD
The risk and its classification are determined based on an evaluation of the potential severity and likeli-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE hood of the product incident. The Product Crisis Team conducts an initial risk assessment, which is con-
FRAMEWORK tinuously adjusted as new information becomes available. A final risk assessment must be performed
once all relevant information has been obtained.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
(ESRS 2)
4. Product crisis and recall actions
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) The Product Crisis Team decides on the necessary actions to be planned and implemented. This decision
always depends on the prior risk assessment, the level of public attention to be achieved and the specific
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) circumstances (such as product distribution levels).
WATER AND MARINE
5. Monitoring and control of actions
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
The Product Crisis Team must monitor, document and evaluate all information related to the response’s
BIODIVERSITY AND scope and effectiveness over a defined period. Additionally, it should review how the product incident
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4) was handled to enhance the Product Crisis and Recall Management Process (PCRMP) for future incidents.

Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model
(ESRS 2, SBM-3_48b)
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
For Henkel, it is essential to conduct business in an ethical and legally compliant manner. Integral to this
commitment is a value-driven corporate culture based on respect, trust and appreciation that consistently
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) opposes discrimination and provides access to equal development opportunities. As part of a materiality
assessment of its own business operations, Henkel has identified various potential negative and positive
BIODIVERSITY AND material impacts related to its business conduct. A corporate culture that undervalues employee well-being,
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
with extended working hours and a lack of work-life balance, can decrease employees’ motivation, job satis-
faction and overall well-being. The lack of whistleblower protection can lead to retaliation, suppressed re-
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5) porting and harmful practices, posing economic and health risks to employees. In contrast, a value-based
corporate culture, including whistleblower protection and ethical business practices, can contribute to a
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) positive work environment, employee well-being and compliance with regulations.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS Henkel is committed to ensuring corporate governance and compliance. The codes, standards and pro-
(ESRS S2)
cesses we have established provide our employees, customers, suppliers and investors, as well as the
communities in which we operate with a clear definition of the ethical and social values we uphold. All stake-
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
holders are encouraged to report any potential misconduct through Henkel’s whistleblower system. We
(ESRS S3)
have established clear due diligence and compliance processes to identify and assess the potential adverse
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS impacts of our business activities. Special protections against retaliation for whistleblowers are in place.
(ESRS S4)
Fairness and objectivity are top priorities in our procedures for pursuing reports of legal violations. To
impart clear rules of conduct to our employees and especially to avoid any conflicts of interest in everyday
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
working situations, we focus on regular training courses and communication actions on compliance issues.

Business conduct policies and corporate culture (G1-1)
SCOPE AND REFERENCE Key contents of the policy (G1-1_7, G1-1_9, G1-1_10a, MDR-P_65a)
FRAMEWORK Corporate culture
Fostering a culture of diversity, equity & inclusion, teamwork, and family spirit are cornerstones of our success.
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
Only in a fair, healthy, and safe work environment will our employees be able to reach their full potential.
(ESRS 2)
It is a mindset that we want to represent and stand for at Henkel, but also beyond in society, having our em-
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) ployees serve as role models for future generations.
We are committed to fostering a respectful and inclusive culture that enables our employees to be their best
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
every day. Henkel is committed to a diverse and inclusive workforce, recognizing it as a key strength that
WATER AND MARINE fosters talent retention, innovation, and understanding of stakeholders. The Company promotes a culture of
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) trust, teamwork, and respect, with zero tolerance for harassment, bullying, or discrimination. We provide our
employees with opportunities equally accessible to all, enabling them to contribute to Henkel and grow
BIODIVERSITY AND
both professionally and personally. Aspects of corporate culture are addressed and discussed at the meet-
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
ings of the Management Board and Supervisory Board at least once a year and on an ad-hoc basis.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
Protection of whistleblowers
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
Our ethical and compliant behavior includes strict compliance with whistleblower laws globally. Therefore, all
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) employees, stakeholders and people affected by Henkel’s business activities, are requested to report poten-
tial misconduct. The process adheres to the global whistleblower laws, ensuring a confidential and fair pro-
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS cess for reporting and addressing potential misconduct. Those who report in good faith are protected and
(ESRS S2)
any kind of retaliation against them will not be tolerated. The reporting channels for whistleblowers are
communicated to our employees and external stakeholders in an appropriate manner, including via the in-
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
(ESRS S3) tranet and the internet.
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
(ESRS S4)
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
HENKEL SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2024 310
Henkel is committed to handling whistleblower reports with the highest level of care, promptness, and confi-
FOREWORD
dentiality, in full compliance with relevant data protection laws. Access to these reports is limited to the nec-
SCOPE AND REFERENCE essary members of Henkel’s Compliance Group (both local and global) and, when needed, other relevant
FRAMEWORK departments or external professional investigators/auditors for the purpose of conducting an investigation
and taking appropriate action. Henkel ensures that all whistleblower reports are properly recorded and doc-
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
umented. The investigation process is guided by principles of procedural fairness and objectivity, leading
(ESRS 2)
to an impartial decision. Throughout the process, individuals involved are presumed innocent until evidence
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) proves a violation has occurred. Henkel takes a strong stance against confirmed misconduct, which could
result in disciplinary actions against those responsible.
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
These principles described above are of fundamental importance for internal collaboration at Henkel and
WATER AND MARINE also extend to interactions with suppliers and business partners. Henkel’s compliance culture involves con-
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
tinuous monitoring and improvement of the compliance process. Our global compliance management sys-
tem was once again audited by external auditors in 2022 based on IDW PS 980 assurance standard and under
BIODIVERSITY AND
due consideration of ISO 37301, with respect to the appropriateness, implementation, and effectiveness of
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
the global compliance processes in the areas of competition law and anti-corruption. Henkel has repeatedly
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR and successfully passed this special audit.

Scope (G1-1_7, MDR-P_65b)
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1) The provisions in this policy apply to the Henkel Group worldwide, including all operations and subsidiaries
as well as their upstream and downstream value chain. No business units, regions, specific activities or stake-
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
holders are excluded.
(ESRS S2)
Accountability for the implementation (G1-1_7, MDR-P_65c)
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
The Henkel Executive Board bears the overall responsibility for the Compliance organization, which ensures
(ESRS S3)
adherence to global laws and policies. The Company-wide Compliance organization is led by the Chief Com-
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS pliance Officer (CCO) with global responsibility. The CCO is supported by the Corporate Compliance Office
(ESRS S4) with the Head of Corporate Compliance & Privacy Protection Officer, and by an interdisciplinary Compliance
& Risk Committee, as well as by 50 local compliance officers worldwide. The various tasks and responsibili-
BUSINESS CONDUCT (ESRS G1)
ties are clearly defined.

The compliance structure functions across three key areas: prevention, detection, and response. It is respon-
FOREWORD
sible for all compliance activities, coordinates training and education, monitors compliance with internal and
SCOPE AND REFERENCE external regulations, and supports the development and implementation of globally binding internal stand-
FRAMEWORK ards. Compliance issues and potential violations are regularly presented to the Management Board, the Audit
Committee of the Supervisory Board, the Shareholders’ Committee, and other local entities.

Policy for training (G1-1_10g)
FOREWORD
To impart clear rules of conduct to our employees, and especially to avoid any conflicts of interest in everyday
SCOPE AND REFERENCE working situations, we focus on regular training courses and communication measures. Our managers play a
FRAMEWORK key role with regard to compliance. Given their position within the Company, they bear a special responsibility
to set an example for their staff. For this reason, all of our managers across the globe must participate in our
GENERAL DISCLOSURES
mandatory Compliance eLearning program and attend relevant training courses, usually several onboarding
(ESRS 2)
courses as well as at least one specific training per year. The program addresses many different compliance
CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1) topics, such as data protection, competition law and anti-corruption.

Business activities
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
Henkel AG & Co. KGaA is operationally active in the two business units Adhesive Technologies and Consumer
WATER AND MARINE Brands, as well as being the parent Company of the Henkel Group.
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
As such, it is responsible for defining and pursuing Henkel’s corporate objectives and also for the manage-
BIODIVERSITY AND ment, control and monitoring of Group-wide activities, including risk management and the allocation of
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
resources. The operating business of Henkel AG & Co. KGaA represents only a portion of the business activ-
ity of the entire Henkel Group and is managed across the Group by the business units.
RESOURCE USE AND CIRCULAR
ECONOMY (ESRS E5)
Matters relating to sustainability at Henkel AG & Co. KGaA are characterized by its own operating activities
and those of its subsidiaries. Regarding sustainability issues and the risk position of Henkel AG & Co. KGaA,
OWN WORKFORCE (ESRS S1)
it is therefore appropriate to refer to the group statement.
VALUE CHAIN WORKERS
(ESRS S2) Non-financial report
Pursuant to Section 289b of the German Commercial Code (HGB), we did not employ a framework for our
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES non-financial report with respect to Henkel AG & Co. KGaA because our stakeholders consider the European
(ESRS S3)
Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS) reporting to be relevant to the Henkel Group. Henkel AG & Co.
KGaA is the parent Company of the Henkel Group and is therefore responsible for all corporate decisions.
CONSUMERS AND END-USERS
Thus, with regard to the content of the non-financial statement for Henkel AG & Co. KGaA in accordance
(ESRS S4)
with Section 289b of the HGB, reference can be made to the declaration relating to the Henkel Group.

CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
Inherent Limitations in the Preparation of the Combined Non-Financial Reporting
POLLUTION (ESRS E2)
The relevant German statutory legal and European regulations contain wording and terms that are still
subject to considerable interpretation uncertainties and for which no authoritative, comprehensive inter-
WATER AND MARINE
RESOURCES (ESRS E3) pretations have yet been published. As such wording and terms may be interpreted differently by regulators
or courts, the legal conformity of measurements or evaluations of sustainability matters based on these
BIODIVERSITY AND interpretations is uncertain.
ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
These inherent limitations also affect the assurance engagement on the Combined Non-Financial Reporting.

Non-Financial Reporting

FOREWORD

SCOPE AND REFERENCE

GENERAL DISCLOSURES the Combined Non-Financial Reporting.
(ESRS 2)  identify disclosures where a material misstatement due to fraud or error is likely to arise, design and

CLIMATE CHANGE (ESRS E1)
conclusion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than the risk
POLLUTION (ESRS E2) of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery,

WATER AND MARINE of not detecting a material misstatement within value chain information from sources not under the
RESOURCES (ESRS E3)
control of the company (value chain information) is generally higher than the risk of not detecting a material
misstatement of value chain information from sources under the control of the company, as both the
BIODIVERSITY AND

ECOSYSTEMS (ESRS E4)
on direct access to the sources of value chain information.
